These are bosonic modes. Return closes in a single traversal. Multiple instances can occupy the same state without contradiction. Accumulation is permitted because no anchoring strain is held. In familiar encoding, this appears as integer spin and Bose–Einstein statistics.

## Transport Modes

The photon is pure transport. No anchoring cost is paid. Compatibility propagates without fixing identity. This appears as a massless gauge field. Gluons transport color alignment within partially anchored composites.

Weak bosons couple asymmetrically to lifted closure windows. Their transport is tied to alignment, and anchoring cost appears as mass. A scalar mode adjusts the reference alignment itself. In standard encoding, this appears as the Higgs.

## Statistics as Closure Type

Statistics is not an added postulate. Anchored lifted closure produces exclusion. Non-anchoring transport permits accumulation. Spin–statistics correspondence has a natural closure explanation once it is encoded in standard field language.

## Combination and Emergence

Closure strain may be redistributed. When multiple identities share incompatibility, a higher-level stable configuration may emerge. Composites are negotiated admissibility. The anchored and transport distinction persists across scales. Atoms, nuclei, and collective modes inherit closure type through total spin, symmetry, and effective collective state.

Fermionic or bosonic behavior is determined by inherited closure, not by substance.

## Constraint

Admissibility excludes unlimited freely propagating identity. It also excludes persistent locality violation and the indefinite retention of every distinction. Termination, equivalence, and stable return enforce these limits.

## Toward Non-Factorization

So far configurations have been treated as separable. Anchors persist independently. Transport mediates compatibility. But separability is not guaranteed. When admissible configurations must be described jointly, closure may no longer factor. Compatibility becomes relational.

This is the origin of entanglement.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Pauli exclusion: the standard encoding of anchored lifted return**

In standard quantum theory, identical fermions have antisymmetric states under exchange. This has a direct consequence: two identical fermions cannot occupy the same quantum state. This explains why electrons fill shells, why matter has volume, and why degeneracy pressure can support compact stars. Bosons admit symmetric states and can accumulate in a single mode, enabling coherence phenomena such as lasers and superfluidity. This is the familiar encoding of a deeper structural distinction: anchored lifted return excludes, while non-anchoring transport accumulates.

</div>

# Entanglement as Shared State

## When Independent Description Fails

Description has so far proceeded as if reality could be decomposed into independent parts. Each place realizes the same internal world locally. Some realizations anchor identity. Others transport neutrality. Local admissibility appeared sufficient.

But global admissibility imposes a stricter condition.

Sometimes the universe cannot be described as a collection of separable pieces. This is not a breakdown of locality. It is a failure of factorization.

## Locality Remains

Nothing connects distant places. Nothing propagates outside admissible channels. Each place continues to realize the shared internal world locally. Return and neutrality remain intact. What changes is not propagation, but admissible description.

## Failure of Factorization

Global admissibility requires that multiple local realizations remain jointly compatible. In many cases, local descriptions may be assigned independently. But interaction can make independence inadmissible: a single joint encoding becomes required. Finite coherence capacity does not create entanglement. It limits how far shared encoding can extend and remain recoverable under embedding.

The incompatibility is structural, not dynamical. Joint admissibility has become the constraint. Independent descriptions are no longer admissible.

## Joint Admissibility

When factorization fails, description lifts into joint encoding. Instead of assigning separate states, a single joint encoding is required. This encoding does not sit between places. It sits in the admissible description of the shared internal world. Local realization continues. But independent selection does not.

The description is shared. Space is not. Shared here means a non-factorizing admissible description, or equivalently a constraint on the joint configuration. It is not a medium between places.

## Distinction from Gauge

Gauge symmetry removes distinctions that never mattered. Entanglement removes separability where distinctions do matter. Gauge identifies redundancy. Entanglement enforces joint compatibility.

## Extent and Fragility

Joint admissibility is constrained. As the entangled domain expands, coherence margins narrow. Finite capacity limits how widely shared encoding can remain stable and recoverable under embedding. Large-scale entanglement is fragile because admissibility tolerances diminish. Protected or engineered regimes can carry entanglement across long ranges. The limit concerns durable recoverability, not distance alone.

Partition becomes necessary when joint encoding exceeds capacity.

## Stabilization

Entanglement need not dissolve. When shared encoding is embedded within a larger admissible structure (redundancy, symmetry protection, or controlled coupling), closure strain can be redistributed. In such regimes, joint description stabilizes as part of a higher-level coherence. Fragility reflects proximity to admissibility limits, not inevitability of collapse.

Collapse occurs only when admissible continuation itself terminates.

## Transition

Joint encoding cannot always persist. There are regimes where shared admissibility remains possible only while commitment is deferred. When deferral becomes inadmissible, joint description cannot be carried forward. Description must restrict itself to one admissible continuation. This restriction is selection.

Collapse is the restriction that occurs when joint admissible continuation fails on that domain.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Bell tests: correlations that cannot be reduced to separable local bookkeeping**

Bell test experiments provide the concrete mechanism-level evidence that entanglement is not ignorance about separate parts.

Prepare pairs of particles in an entangled state. Measure each particle with one of several possible settings chosen independently at spacelike separation. Compare the joint statistics. If the outcomes were determined by local hidden variables carried by each particle, with the usual independence and factorization assumptions, the correlations would satisfy Bell inequalities. Experimentally, the correlations violate those inequalities.

No faster-than-light messaging is involved. No usable signal is sent. The pair behaves as a single state for purposes of prediction: the correct ledger is joint, not separable.

Entanglement is shared admissibility in precisely this restricted sense. The description does not factor into independent local identities without losing predictive closure.

</div>

# Collapse as Branch Restriction

No physical system exists in isolation.

Every local realization participates in a larger admissible structure. Shared admissibility is the condition that makes continuation possible. This is not only entanglement. It is the general condition for any shared description: whenever multiple degrees of freedom must remain mutually compatible, continuation is constrained jointly. As long as shared admissibility can be preserved, continuation remains possible.

An interaction may preserve that shared admissibility. It may also push a domain past the point where alternatives can still be carried together. Collapse becomes the effective description when shared admissibility fails on that domain.

## Termination of Continuation

Under finite coherence capacity, description cannot carry unlimited unresolved distinction and remain stable under reuse. There are configurations for which continuation cannot remain admissible. No refinement preserves compatibility. No redistribution absorbs mismatch. At that boundary, the shared description cannot be extended over the affected domain.

Admissible continuation terminates.

This termination is structural.

## Collapse as Restriction

When admissible continuation terminates, deferred commitment ends on that domain. A finite set of admissible continuations remains. For the affected stabilized domain, only one remains reusable. Collapse is this restriction: the reduction of admissible description to a single continuation where shared admissibility has failed. Collapse is domain-local. Outside the affected region, shared description may persist.

## Irreversibility

Below stabilization thresholds, reversibility can persist: weak measurements, echoes, and recoherence can still recover deferred structure. Once an interaction has left a durable macroscopic trace, discarded continuations are no longer admissible within that domain. They are not erased. They are structurally excluded from the description that can be reused. This irreversibility is not explained solely by noise, dissipation, or an act of looking.

Collapse unfolds along an ordering already present. It does not create the arrow of time.

## Probability

At the boundary of termination, multiple admissible continuations may remain. Each corresponds to a basin of admissible extension beyond that boundary. Basin geometry gives the closure-side account of outcome weighting. Standard quantum theory supplies the Born rule used in calculation.

## Collapse and the Universe

Collapse is a local restriction.

The universe-scale statement is not that an outcome is chosen globally, but that outcomes may be restricted locally without breaking global admissibility. Not every alternative must remain jointly extendable. The realized pattern of restrictions must remain completable as one history. Collapse is therefore not a global event. It is a permitted local operation inside a globally constrained closure.

## What Collapse Enables

Without restriction at termination, no stable record could persist. No hierarchy could reliably scale. No macroscopic identity could remain coherent.

Collapse is not a defect.

It is the structural restriction by which admissibility remains coherent when continuation cannot remain shared.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Measurement interaction: branch restriction at stable distinction**

Von Neumann’s measurement postulate is the standard formal encoding of collapse: a rule for updating the state when an outcome is obtained. It is the ideal projective case; generalized measurements extend the same operational logic. Under unitary evolution, a quantum state evolves smoothly and preserves superpositions. A measurement interaction is an ordinary interaction arranged so that alternatives couple to stable, separable outcomes. When that coupling crosses the stabilization threshold, the usable description restricts to an outcome-consistent state, with probabilities given by the Born rule.

Operationally, the key fact is stable distinction. When an interaction produces a macroscopic record that is stable and accessible, continuing to use the pre-measurement superposition ceases to be admissible. Future predictions must be made relative to the recorded outcome. Collapse is the transition from an unresolved description to an outcome-specific one on the affected domain. The record is the stabilized footprint of an interaction that has crossed threshold.

Deferred commitment ends when a shared set of alternatives can no longer be carried and one continuation becomes the history that can be reused.

</div>

# Dark Matter as Persistence Without Visibility

Dark matter is not introduced here as a new ingredient. It is introduced as a pressure on description. Once collapse and admissibility are in view, the closure reading suggests that not everything that contributes to global compatibility must appear as a locally anchored identity. Some contributions persist only as what gravity can read: curvature without visibility, structure without luminous record-forming channels.

## Closure Interpretation

In closure language, dark names a role before it names a substance. It names a mode of persistence under projection. Anchored matter is persistence with stable return. It reinstates identity locally under reuse, couples strongly to record formation, and produces durable visible structure. A different mode is possible.

Some contributions persist in curvature bookkeeping while remaining weakly coupled to the record-forming channels that stabilize externally visible identity. In such a regime, what persists need not be a locally anchored object description. It can be an ensemble-level invariant: a statistically persistent contribution to compatibility even when non-gravitational anchoring is absent, inaccessible, or irrelevant. Finer internal realizations may still exist beneath projection. The interface reads only what remains admissible there. Dark matter is then projected persistence. Gravity can respond to stable aggregate constraint even when locally visible particle identity is not available at the interface.

## What Remains Open

Dark matter may still be particulate. WIMPs, axions, sterile neutrinos, and other non-baryonic candidates remain possible. The structural point is narrower. Whatever dark matter is internally, its defining external role is that it persists gravitationally without appearing as anchored identity in the record-forming channels. Baseline cosmological fits, particle candidates, and modified-gravity comparisons remain part of the open ledger.

## What Comes Next

Dark matter shows that persistence has modes. Some contributions anchor locally. Some remain legible primarily through curvature. A different large-scale discrepancy also appears in the standard account: late-time acceleration. The next chapter treats dark energy as that companion case: late-time acceleration in the baseline description, and global capacity exhaustion in closure terms.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Dark matter: baseline $`\Lambda`$CDM**

Dark matter is inferred as the common remainder across multiple ledgers. Galaxy rotation curves require more gravitating mass than starlight accounts for. Gravitational lensing maps the same excess mass where little light is present. In the baseline $`\Lambda`$CDM fit, the cosmic microwave background and large-scale structure strongly favor an early non-baryonic component. In the simplest successful baseline, this component is effectively cold and weakly self-interacting.

In the baseline picture, dark matter clusters gravitationally and forms extended halos. It does not couple electromagnetically in any appreciable way, so it does not radiate or cool efficiently. Baryons fall into these wells, dissipate, and condense, producing luminous galaxies.

</div>

# Dark Energy as Capacity at the Largest Scale

Dark energy first appears here as a discrepancy in bookkeeping. On the largest scales, the universe expands in a way that cannot be accounted for by matter and radiation alone.

## Closure Interpretation

In closure language, dark energy can be read as a limit on global admissibility. As structure forms, local anchoring stabilizes identities. Compatibility bookkeeping extends across regions.

But global compatibility is not free.

As anchoring thins and accessibility stretches, maintaining coherent global identification becomes more costly. Global bookkeeping compatibility can no longer be maintained at the same resolution everywhere at once. When global reconciliation strain reaches its limit, the admissible structural response is not to destroy anchored identities, but to increase separation. Expansion becomes a way to reduce global incompatibility pressure. What appears in effective equations as a cosmological constant can be read structurally as saturation of relational capacity at the largest scale.

## Why This Is Still Setup

This is still setup. It assigns a role before a detailed cosmological mechanism, inflationary model, or specific scalar field is chosen. Dark energy names the regime in which global spatial bookkeeping is capacity-limited, so separation becomes the admissible way to preserve overall coherence. Standard cosmology still carries this through effective parameters whose detailed physical origin remains open.

## What Comes Next

Dark matter and dark energy together clarify the space of possibilities. Some persistence anchors identity. Some persistence shapes curvature without visibility. Some limits appear only globally. Once these modes are separated, it becomes meaningful to follow cosmic history as a sequence of regimes.

The next chapter steps back from these consequences and marks the transition from necessity to cosmology.

That is where the historical narrative begins.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Dark energy: baseline $`\Lambda`$CDM**

Late-time cosmic acceleration is modeled by introducing a cosmological constant or vacuum energy term in the Einstein equations. For a cosmological constant, this component contributes a nearly uniform energy density that does not dilute significantly with expansion. It dominates the energy budget at late cosmological times, driving accelerated expansion. Current large-scale surveys test whether the effective dark-energy behavior is truly constant or evolves with cosmic time.

</div>

# Interlude: From Necessity to Cosmology

Up to this point, no cosmological history has been assumed. Time, space, gravity, quantization, gauge structure, collapse, dark matter, and dark energy have been introduced as structural consequences of finite admissibility under projection.

Nothing new was added to the universe.

What changed was what became unavoidable once internal realization must remain compatible with external identification under finite capacity. The purpose of the previous chapters was not to tell a story about what happened first. It was to isolate what must already be in place for any story to be describable at all. That work is now complete.

Necessity has supplied the grammar. Cosmology supplies the order in which the grammar becomes historical.

## What Has Been Completed

We now have:

- an internal realization domain, extended and not directly visible,

- an interface at which identity must be re-identified,

- discreteness as survivorship when continuity fails,

- closure requirements for reuse under finite capacity,

- a lifted return structure that makes stable identity possible,

- gauge symmetry as local equivalence and compatibility bookkeeping,

- curvature as global compatibility geometry,

- collapse as termination of admissible continuation when records must form,

- dark matter as persistence that contributes to curvature without visible anchoring,

- dark energy as saturation of global bookkeeping capacity.

These are not separate mechanisms. They are the minimal structural inventory required for a universe to remain describable once closure is incomplete.

## What Has Not Yet Begun

A universe is not only a structure. It is a history.

Nothing above has yet said:

- when any regime dominates,

- how global conditions change,

- how temperature and entropy evolve,

- how anchoring becomes viable,

- how structure formation proceeds.

Those are historical questions. They cannot be answered until the structural inventory is fixed.

# Diagonal Embedding: The Onset of Cosmological History

The closure logic of the proto-spinor now receives a cosmological reading. The formal steps do not change. The staging shifts. This is a staging of realization: how the same constraints appear once a single domain has to hold them.

## Before Physics

Begin, in reconstructed order, with the weakest scaffold that can still support locality. An adjacency structure can exist without metric, without dynamics, and before law is available as reusable description. It is stable as next-to. It is not yet stable as physics. Nothing forces one continuation rather than another. No distinction survives repeated use as a rule.

The scaffold is maximal deferral: possibility without commitment.

## The First Diagonal Embedding

The first forcing step is diagonal embedding. Each external location is no longer treated as purely pointlike. It is paired with a local internal realization. This does not add a second space. It specifies an identification rule: each outer point is realized together with a local internal fiber.

Locality is preserved. Nothing global is inserted. The outer scaffold remains what it was. What changes is return. A location is now realized in two linked ways: as an external point of identification, and as the label of a locally extended internal realization. Return can close externally while internal distinction remains available.

Mismatch becomes unavoidable.

## From Mismatch to Closure

Mismatch is not an anomaly. It is the retained obstruction that forces closure roles. If internal distinctions are erased, refinement collapses. If they are preserved without repair, reuse fails. If repair stores memory, capacity accumulates.

Closure is therefore forced. Return must be lifted. Equivalence must be transportable. Termination must occur where refinement would violate admissibility. Only after these repairs can identity become countable under repetition.

Only after these repairs can anything function as law.

## The Immediate Consequence

Diagonal embedding makes mismatch ubiquitous. External identity must return exactly. Internal distinction must remain available. Once coexistence is required across the domain, compatibility cannot be maintained by a single pass.

Correction must iterate.

This iteration is not yet cosmology. It is closure: the repeated reconciliation required for any stable global regime to become admissible.

## The Start of Cosmology

Once diagonal embedding is fixed, a global history becomes describable. Only then, in the reconstructed order, does it become meaningful to describe the early universe as a sequence of regimes. Inflation is not the origin of closure. It is the first historical regime in which the already-established structure can be realized globally without immediate collapse. At that point, cosmology can begin.

*The story now starts.*

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Diagonal embedding: one 3D world, three bookkeeping geometries**

A rotating ring gives a concrete example of loop-dependent bookkeeping. Put identical clocks all around the ring, and attempt Einstein synchronization by exchanging light signals between neighbors. Locally it works: each pair of neighbors can agree on a midpoint time using round-trip light travel. But the global test is different: synchronize all the way around the ring and return to where you started.

Going around the loop and coming back does *not* reproduce the original clock setting. This is not a technological defect; it is the Sagnac time-lag: rotation turns “return” into an observable holonomy. The attempt to make a single-valued global time forces three distinct structural roles to appear:

- **Lens (equivalence / gauge):** even before anything fails, there is a built-in freedom: you may offset all clock readings by the same constant and nothing physical changes. This “choice of zero” is redundancy. A global description must transport equivalence consistently across space.

- **Circle (return / holonomy):** the loop test $`\text{(walk around)}\rightarrow\text{(come back)}`$ produces a nontrivial remainder, a genuine measurable loop fact. The world remembers what happened on return. This is the circle geometry: bookkeeping that only exists because you can close a path.

- **Nil (cut / termination surface):** to produce a *single-valued* time coordinate anyway, you must introduce a controlled termination surface where the would-be global time is allowed to jump by exactly the loop remainder. You can place that cut surface anywhere as a gauge choice, but you cannot remove the need for it. The cut is a defect in extendability: a controlled termination of smooth patching.

This is diagonal embedding in concrete form. You do not replace a 3D world by three different worlds. You take the *same* 3D scaffold and embed it into three simultaneous bookkeeping geometries: a lens structure that carries equivalence, a circle structure that records return, and a nil structure that marks where global extendability must be broken to remain coherent. The onset of cosmological history is the moment this triple bookkeeping becomes globally usable: only then can “the universe” support a consistent ordered narrative across all of space, rather than merely local updates that cannot be reconciled on return.

</div>

# Inflation: The First Historical Realization

In this reconstructed order, inflation is where constraint becomes historical: the regime in which the system must respond everywhere at once. Locality and finiteness still hold. Admissibility now has to be maintained globally. After diagonal embedding, mismatch is no longer local. External identity must return exactly, while internal distinction must persist.

A single global composition fails. Compatibility cannot be achieved in one pass. Correction must iterate. The inflationary regime is read here as that first global iteration and the requirement that it succeed quickly enough for later structure to persist.

## Reference and Cancellation

There exists an ideal limit in which internal structure cancels perfectly at its boundary.

In that limit:

- no mismatch accumulates,

- no retained distinction is required,

- no temporal ordering is enforced.

This limit is not a historical phase. It is a consistency condition: a reference against which persistence cost is measured. In particle language, it resembles the role played by vacuum structure. It defines symmetry. It does not define history.

## Why Exact Cancellation Fails Globally

Exact cancellation may be defined locally. It cannot be composed globally under finite capacity. Once internal distinction is unavoidable, comparison requires bookkeeping. Bookkeeping retains distinction. Retained distinction breaks exact cancellation.

The failure is not dynamical decay. It is non-composability.

## The Onset of Self-Reference

Iteration therefore cannot be avoided.

At maximal realization density, comparison spans the entire domain. When comparison must be global, realization attempts to close itself by referencing itself. But global comparison has no natural stopping point. Resolution demands yet more resolution. This is self-reference under finite capacity. It cannot close.

Global comparability without a scale boundary generates unbounded bookkeeping demand. Under finite capacity, that demand overwhelms composability.

## The First Irreversible Moment

History begins here when the first distinction must be retained. Once something must be kept, ordering becomes unavoidable. Symmetry remains in law. It breaks in realization. From that point onward, some continuations cannot be restored within the same admissible chart.

Irreversibility appears.

This is the arrow of time: not imposed, enforced by retained distinction.

## Why Expansion Is Required

Self-reference cannot be repaired by local correction because the incompatibility is global. A scale boundary must be introduced. Expansion supplies that boundary. Separation limits what must be compared at once. It reduces the density of mutual constraint.

It restores composability.

Dilution is not preference. It is the required response in this reconstruction.

## Why Expansion Accelerates

Compatibility strain propagates at finite rate. If separation grows too slowly, comparison continues to enforce global bookkeeping. For the role assigned here, ordinary expansion does not suffice. Separation must grow faster than incompatibility accumulates. A brief epoch of accelerated expansion provides the required dilution.

This regime is called inflation.

## Seeding Structure

Inflation smooths curvature but does not eliminate fluctuation. Quantum fluctuations stretched beyond the horizon are treated effectively as classical primordial perturbations after horizon exit and later evolution. Residual mismatch, frozen at super-horizon scales, becomes the seed of later structure. Inflation does not create structure from nothing. It preserves irreducible variation within an expanding background.

## Field Dynamics

Inflation does not suspend physical law. In many standard inflationary models, it is modeled by a scalar field whose potential energy dominates and drives accelerated expansion. The field equations remain valid. The regime changes. No specific inflaton or potential is yet part of the observational ledger.

## Inflation and Late-Time Acceleration

Accelerated expansion appears again at late times. That later regime is treated separately as dark energy. The distinction is structural: early acceleration resolves excessive density, while late acceleration responds to thinning anchoring.

## What Inflation Accomplishes

In standard cosmology, inflation addresses three linked problems:

- it renders spatial curvature negligible within the observable domain,

- it makes large-scale uniformity dynamically plausible,

- it stretches quantum fluctuations into classical perturbations.

In the closure reconstruction, it also fixes the direction of historical continuation: after retained distinction appears, later states are not simple reversals of earlier ones. Without such an accelerated early regime, the observed near-flatness and large-scale homogeneity would require extreme fine-tuning. Inflation remains one of the simplest dynamical regimes known in which history becomes globally admissible.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Flatness Problem: Why Inflation Is Invoked**

In standard Friedmann expansion, any deviation from spatial flatness grows in relative importance over time. To observe the universe today as extremely close to flat would require the early universe to have been extraordinarily fine-tuned. Inflation provides a dynamical alternative. During accelerated expansion, the curvature term in the Friedmann equation is rapidly diluted. In suitable inflationary models, a wide range of initial curvature values are driven toward near-flatness within the observable domain.

Flatness becomes an attractor, not an accident.

</div>

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Horizon Problem: Why Distant Regions Look Alike**

The cosmic microwave background is nearly uniform across the sky. Regions separated by many degrees were not in causal contact in a simple non-inflationary thermal history. Without a prior mechanism, there is no time for them to equilibrate. They cannot exchange signals fast enough to agree on a common temperature before photons decouple. Inflation supplies a dynamical way to make this uniformity admissible.

A region that was in causal contact before inflation is rapidly stretched to scales far larger than the post-inflation horizon. In the inflationary explanation, the observable universe is assembled from a patch that already shared thermal conditions. Mechanically, inflation does not transmit new information faster than light. It changes which regions were ever required to agree in the first place by stretching an initially connected domain into the observable sky.

</div>

# Post-Inflation: Flat Complementary Bookkeeping

Inflation does not end because expansion stops. Structurally, it ends when the overload condition relaxes. The conditions that required rapid dilution have been satisfied. Global incompatibility no longer forces immediate correction. What remains is extension without collapse.

This is the first regime in which coexistence becomes dynamically stable. Local processes can unfold without forcing universal reconciliation.

## Near-Flat Geometry

Post-inflation geometry is nearly flat. Flatness does not mean absence of curvature. It means curvature no longer dominates the large-scale expansion history in the effective cosmological model. Local curvature may arise wherever mass–energy is present. But global collapse does not follow.

Expansion continues without compulsory global correction. The background is stable enough to host variation.

## Reheating

Inflation ends through energy transfer. In many inflationary models, the sector that drove accelerated expansion does not vanish. It relaxes by transferring energy into other degrees of freedom. Energy stored in the inflationary sector is converted into ordinary relativistic excitations. A hot plasma fills the universe.

This process is called reheating. Reheating does not create structure. It creates the conditions under which structure might later survive.

## Radiation Without Anchoring

After reheating, energy density is dominated by relativistic excitation. Fields fluctuate. Creation and annihilation proceed efficiently. Particle and antiparticle populations are nearly symmetric, except for small asymmetries that later become decisive. In the earliest high-temperature part of this regime, there are no atoms. No nuclei. No long-lived composite identities.

Radiation gravitates. Relativistic matter gravitates. All stress–energy participates.

But nothing yet anchors identity.

## Dark Matter in the Historical Narrative

Dark matter’s role has already been assigned: persistence that contributes to curvature without entering the record-forming channels that make matter luminous. In the standard observational ledger, it is the effectively non-luminous, non-baryonic component that clusters gravitationally, shapes halos, and couples at most very weakly to electromagnetism in the baseline picture. It is not radiation. It is gravitational persistence that remains externally legible even when electromagnetic anchoring is absent. Its influence becomes decisive once structure formation begins.

## What Exists in This Regime

At this point, the universe contains:

- a near-flat expanding background,

- a hot relativistic plasma,

- non-baryonic dark matter,

- and no stable atomic or chemical structure.

Atoms do not yet exist. Chemistry does not yet operate. Durable baryonic anchoring has not yet reached its later thresholds.

That requires cooling.

## What Comes Next

Post-inflation establishes a stable background. It permits persistence to compete with cancellation. What now determines the next transitions is not curvature, but disturbance density. That quantity is temperature.

<div class="minipage">

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Reheating: the transfer from vacuum dominance to plasma**

During inflation, the universe is dominated by vacuum-like energy. In many inflationary models, this is represented by a scalar sector slowly evolving in its potential. As inflation ends in those models, that sector oscillates about its minimum. Those oscillations are unstable. Through decay or parametric resonance, energy stored in the inflationary sector is transferred into ordinary relativistic degrees of freedom.

This process is called reheating. Reheating fills the universe with a hot plasma of interacting fields. It sets the hot starting conditions for the subsequent thermal history. From this point onward, cosmic evolution is governed not by vacuum dominance, but by radiation and later matter. Reheating does not yet create stable structure. It initializes the regime in which such structure can eventually emerge.

</div>

</div>

# Temperature: Conditions for Persistence

With the bookkeeping background established, local processes can now compete with cancellation. Curvature remains present, but it no longer dominates which regimes can persist. What now controls persistence is disturbance density. That quantity is temperature. Nothing new is being defined here. Only the arena changes: from structural necessity to the expanding plasma in which realizations either stabilize or fail.

## What Temperature Is

Temperature does not describe what exists. It describes how difficult it is for anything to remain what it is. Thermodynamically, temperature is the state variable that tracks the distribution of energy across accessible degrees of freedom. Structurally, energy is also the cost of maintaining a configuration. Temperature summarizes how that cost is distributed across accessible degrees of freedom. When temperature is high, typical fluctuations are large. Interaction is unavoidable. Reconfiguration is frequent. Cancellation proceeds efficiently. Structure cannot hold. When temperature falls, interaction weakens. Reconfiguration slows. Persistence becomes possible.

## Temperature and Binding

High temperature overwhelms binding. Fluctuations exceed structural thresholds. Composite identity dissolves. As temperature drops below specific energy scales in separate later transitions, binding becomes favorable.

Nuclei can assemble. Atoms can form. Photons can decouple. Structure can stabilize. Temperature does not create structure. It determines whether structure survives.

## Toward Entropy

Temperature measures present disturbance. Entropy records cumulative loss: how many continuations have already closed. They are distinct. But in cosmology, their evolution becomes inseparable.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Freeze-out: when expansion outruns interaction**

In an expanding universe, interaction must keep pace with dilution. If reactions occur much faster than expansion, equilibrium is maintained. Creation and annihilation balance. When expansion becomes faster than the relevant interactions, reactions can no longer keep up. Abundances stop tracking equilibrium. They freeze out.

Freeze-out-like departures from equilibrium enter:

- baryon asymmetry persistence after baryogenesis,

- neutrino decoupling,

- dark matter abundance in thermal-relic models,

- and reaction departures during light-element synthesis.

Freeze-out marks the transition from reversible fluctuation to persistent asymmetry.

</div>

# Entropy: Loss of Admissible Continuation

Temperature measures present disturbance. Entropy measures accumulated loss. They are not the same quantity. Both are necessary to understand cosmological history.

## What Entropy Is

Entropy is not only loose disorder or subjective ignorance. Here entropy is treated structurally. It measures how much continuation is no longer admissible within a single stable description. As realization proceeds under finite capacity, some paths close. Once stabilized as durable records, reopening them means changing the regime itself.

Entropy names this cumulative contraction of admissible continuation.

## Irreversibility

Reversal is permitted in principle. Under finite capacity, it cannot be maintained universally. As history unfolds, some possibilities are realized, others excluded. Continuous families collapse to discrete outcomes. Admissible branches separate.

Within a given effective admissible chart, this loss is irreversible. Entropy does not imply destruction of underlying structure. It reflects loss of reconstructibility within effective description.

## Entropy and Geometry

Entropy tracks loss of recoverable continuation in time. Horizons enforce loss of recoverable continuation in space. What entropy accumulates temporally, horizons impose geometrically. Neither need be read as literal annihilation. Both limit accessibility and reconstructibility.

## Entropy Within Ordering

Entropy does not create time. It accumulates along it.

In time:

- excluded paths cannot be restored without altering ordering,

- bookkeeping cannot be undone within the same stabilized domain.

In space:

- compatibility channels terminate,

- extension narrows,

- global comparability thins.

Entropy measures contraction. It does not generate order.

## Entropy After Inflation

Inflation establishes a large-scale effective background. A common confusion belongs here. The early universe was hot, yet low-entropy in the gravitational sense. Thermal entropy can be large while gravitational entropy is small. A nearly uniform mass distribution is smooth and highly constrained.

It contains little of the clumping, collapse, and horizon structure through which gravity realizes vast numbers of distinct microhistories. The early plasma is thermally busy, but geometrically simple.

## Why Entropy Increases

Entropy increases because finite capacity forces selection. When coherence must be maintained, not all continuations can remain compatible. Some paths are abandoned. Some correlations are lost to reconstruction. This is not drift toward disorder. It is the cost of persistence under constraint. Standard thermodynamics describes the same direction through coarse-grained state counting given low-entropy boundary conditions.

## What Comes Next

Temperature governs interaction. Entropy governs irreversibility. Together they define the regime in which high disturbance density restricts persistence. That regime is radiation domination.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Comoving Entropy: Irreversibility Under Expansion**

In an expanding universe, the same physical processes occur, but the background conditions change continuously. Expansion dilutes energy density. It also dilutes local entropy density. What matters for cosmology is not entropy at a point, but entropy carried along with the expanding flow. If the plasma remains close to equilibrium and no entropy-producing processes occur, the total entropy contained in a comoving region remains approximately constant.

Expansion spreads the contents out, but it does not by itself create new entropy. Irreversibility enters when equilibrium fails. Freeze-out, annihilation, and phase transitions prevent the system from tracking the equilibrium state appropriate to the new conditions. These events generate additional entropy. Cosmological entropy is therefore not arbitrary disorder. It is the cumulative record of processes that cannot be undone within the same expansion regime.

Adiabatic expansion approximately preserves a comoving entropy budget. Nonequilibrium processes, annihilations, and sector decouplings can produce or redistribute entropy.

</div>

# Radiation Domination: Transport Under Pressure

With temperature high and entropy accumulating, the universe enters its first long-lived thermal regime. Energy density is dominated by relativistic degrees of freedom. Pressure is significant. Curvature responds to radiation. Anchoring cannot stabilize. Interaction overwhelms persistence.

Realization proceeds primarily through transport.

## Transport and Anchoring

Two realization modes are now distinct:

- *Anchoring:* discrete persistence of identity.

- *Transport:* propagation without durable local anchoring.

At high disturbance density, anchoring fails. Repeated interaction dissolves localization. Binding thresholds are exceeded. Transport, by contrast, requires no stable local anchored identity. It carries compatibility through motion alone. Radiation domination is therefore not preference for transport, but suppression of anchoring. At the level of the universe’s budget, transport is the dominant admissible mode.

## Radiation Pressure

Radiation gravitates. But it also exerts pressure. Pressure resists gravitational compression. For relativistic species, pressure is one third of energy density. As long as radiation dominates, photon-baryon coupling and radiation pressure suppress gravitational collapse of ordinary matter.

Structure cannot condense. Fluctuations oscillate.

## Dark Matter During Radiation Domination

Dark matter is present throughout this regime. It contributes curvature. It couples at most very weakly to electromagnetism. It does not experience radiation pressure. Because it lacks pressure support, its density perturbations grow slowly, even while baryons remain locked to photons.

Dark matter therefore prepares the gravitational scaffold upon which later anchoring will occur, with efficient growth waiting until matter-radiation equality.

## The Limit of Transport

Radiation domination is not permanent. As expansion proceeds, radiation energy density dilutes faster than matter. Eventually, pressure weakens. Binding thresholds become reachable. Anchoring becomes dynamically viable.

The next chapter follows cooling: the structural relaxation of disturbance by which anchoring becomes viable.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Baryon Acoustic Oscillations: a frozen sound horizon**

Before recombination, baryons and photons behave as a coupled fluid. Dark matter deepens gravitational wells. Baryons fall inward. Photon pressure pushes outward. This produces acoustic waves in the plasma. The characteristic propagation scale is the sound horizon: the distance a pressure wave can travel before the plasma decouples. When recombination occurs and photons decouple, photon pressure support rapidly weakens. The oscillation no longer propagates. The sound-horizon scale is imprinted into the matter distribution.

Later, this appears as a preferred separation scale in galaxy clustering statistics: baryon acoustic oscillations.

</div>

# Cooling: Structural Relaxation of Disturbance

Radiation domination does not end abruptly. It thins. Transport remains active, but disturbance no longer overwhelms every attempt at persistence. Cooling marks the first structural easing of compulsory reconfiguration. It does not introduce identity. It lowers the threshold at which identity can survive.

## Cooling Is Not Energy Loss

Cooling is not disappearance of energy. It is dilution. As the universe expands, energy density spreads across greater extension. For relativistic species, temperature falls roughly in inverse proportion to the scale factor. Local conservation remains intact. Global total energy in an expanding spacetime is not generally a single conserved quantity. What changes is interaction density.

Realizations are no longer forced to renegotiate themselves at every encounter. Cooling names a shift in how frequently stability is challenged, not what structure exists.

## Disturbance as Constraint

At high disturbance density:

- interactions are frequent,

- binding is disrupted,

- anchoring cannot persist.

As expansion proceeds:

- reaction rates fall,

- compatibility strain redistributes,

- anchoring attempts are no longer immediately erased.

Cooling is structural relaxation. It is not a new force. It is the weakening of enforced reconfiguration.

## The Last Threshold: Recombination

Cooling eventually reaches its final major threshold in the early thermal ladder. Electrons can remain bound to nuclei. Neutral atoms become statistically favored. With the free electron population reduced, the Thomson scattering opacity falls sharply. Transport decouples from matter.

The universe becomes transparent. The cosmic microwave background is the relic of this transition: radiation last scattered across a finite epoch once atomic anchoring becomes admissible.

## Approaching Anchoring

Cooling reduces disturbance density. It does not yet secure durable complexity. Gravitational collapse remains gradual. Dark matter structures deepen. Baryonic matter can now fall into them without being immediately reversed by radiation pressure.

There exists a structural boundary below which anchoring becomes dynamically viable. Cooling approaches that boundary. The next regime concerns what happens when anchoring ceases to be temporary and becomes organizing.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Cooling thresholds: from plasma to atoms**

Cooling is not one event. It is a sequence of thresholds at which new kinds of persistence become admissible.

**Confinement.** At the highest temperatures, quarks and gluons do not form durable composites. As disturbance density falls, they bind into hadrons. Protons and neutrons become meaningful carriers of baryon number.

**Annihilation and residue.** As the universe cools further, baryons and antibaryons annihilate efficiently. If the net baryon number were exactly zero, baryons would not survive as a durable inventory. A tiny residual asymmetry remains, and that residue becomes the matter content that persists. The origin of that asymmetry remains a separate mechanism question.

**Nucleosynthesis.** When conditions allow light nuclei to remain bound, protons and neutrons assemble into helium and trace light elements. This is the first durable nuclear anchoring.

**Recombination and transparency.** Much later, electrons can remain bound to nuclei. Neutral atoms form in large numbers. With the free electron population reduced, photon scattering opacity falls sharply. Radiation decouples and becomes free-streaming.

The cosmic microwave background is the relic of this last threshold: radiation last scattered across a finite epoch once atomic anchoring becomes admissible.

</div>

# Matter Domination: Anchors Over Transport

Identity anchoring has become dynamically viable. Matter domination begins when anchored persistence, not transport, organizes curvature.

No new realization is introduced.

What changes is balance. Anchors now set the structure. Transport no longer sets the regime.

## From Persistence to Organization

In the radiation regime, anchors could exist, but they did not determine global behavior. Pressure smoothed. Oscillation suppressed collapse. Persistence remained local. As expansion proceeds, radiation dilutes faster than matter.

Anchors accumulate. Worldlines lengthen. Interactions become structured rather than transient. Matter domination begins after matter-radiation equality, when persistence organizes curvature and curvature organizes what persists. Baryonic anchoring becomes visible later, after recombination frees matter from photon drag.

## What Domination Means

Matter domination does not eliminate transport.

It means that space and time bookkeeping are now organized around anchored identity.

In time:

- identities persist across long sequences,

- histories become reusable,

- accumulated structure becomes difficult to erase.

In space:

- curvature organizes around persistent concentrations,

- compatibility channels align with anchored structure.

The ingredients are unchanged. The organizing constraint is not.

## Classical Persistence

With anchoring dominant, classical behavior becomes widespread. Quantum structure remains fundamental, but persistent embedding makes deferred commitment expensive. Macroscopic indeterminacy is suppressed through sustained coupling to stable records. Classicality is quantum description under persistent records for ordinary macroscopic observables.

## Virial Stability and Rotation

Collapse does not proceed indefinitely.

As matter contracts, motion rises. Angular momentum is conserved. Rotation strengthens. Velocity dispersion, mergers, and tidal interactions also shape the result. Gravitational systems approach a stable balance in which inward attraction is matched by motion.

Without dissipation, collapse halts in extended configurations. Dark matter cannot radiate efficiently. It stabilizes in diffuse halos. Baryonic matter behaves differently.

It radiates. It cools. It sheds energy while conserving angular momentum. It collapses further. In suitable conditions, disks form. Stars ignite. Planets assemble.

Structure becomes layered.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Jeans instability: when gravity outruns pressure**

A gas cloud resists collapse through pressure. Gravity pulls inward. For sufficiently small regions, pressure responds quickly enough to prevent contraction. Beyond a critical scale, self-gravity overwhelms pressure support. Density perturbations grow instead of dispersing.

Matter domination is the regime in which these unstable regions can grow faster than smoothing processes erase them. Anchoring becomes widespread when gravity outpaces pressure.

</div>

# Gravity: Organizer of Anchored Space

Matter domination makes persistent identity widespread.

Once anchoring becomes common, space can no longer remain indifferent to where identities endure. A second structural role of gravity now comes into view. The same compatibility geometry that keeps bookkeeping mutually admissible now organizes anchored persistence into long-lived regions.

## A Change of Role, Not of Law

Gravity has operated since spacetime became admissible. Its first role was to preserve compatibility when closure could not remain flat.

That role continues.

What changes in matter domination is what gravity organizes. It no longer mainly stabilizes the background. It reorganizes anchored persistence within it. The law does not change. The regime does.

## Anchoring Becomes Spatial

Identity anchors persist across time. They resist reconfiguration. They accumulate history. As anchoring density increases, spatial extension must accommodate continuity, not only propagation. Space is no longer structured primarily to support transport.

It must support accumulated persistence. Gravity is the geometric accommodation of that demand.

## Curvature as Organization

Anchors impose constraints. Compatibility must be maintained in a way that preserves persistence. Where anchoring density is high, greater geometric adjustment is required. Where anchoring thins, less is required. Curvature appears here as this adjustment.

What is described as attraction is the effective appearance of compatibility geometry reorganizing around anchors.

## Dark Matter as Scaffold

Dark matter plays a decisive role. It contributes curvature without participating in electromagnetic anchoring. In the cold-dark-matter baseline, it is effectively non-radiating, non-cooling, and diffuse at large scale. Because of this, dark matter forms extended halos that define stable curvature wells. Visible matter, which does anchor identity and dissipate, settles within these wells.

This distinction governs structure formation.

## Visible Matter as Layered Persistence

Baryonic matter follows different channels. It anchors identity, accumulates history, and resists redistribution. Baryons can radiate, cool, fragment, form stars, and exchange angular momentum. They can shed energy while conserving angular momentum. They collapse further than dark matter can.

Gravity organizes the scaffold. Visible matter condenses within it.

Structure becomes layered.

## Reorganization, Not Creation

Galaxies, clusters, and filaments are not imposed externally. They arise where anchored persistence can reinforce itself within the curvature scaffold. Regions that cross nonlinear, cooling, and feedback thresholds accumulate structure. Regions that do not remain sparse. Gravity does not create identity. It reorganizes where identity becomes durable.

## Limits

Detailed perturbation growth, power spectra, and anisotropy evolution remain within standard cosmological treatment.

The structural claim is narrower: once anchoring dominates, gravity becomes the organizer of anchored space.

## What Comes Next

With curvature organizing persistence, stable spatial regimes emerge. Some regions sustain dense anchoring. Others do not stabilize. Two complementary outcomes follow: galaxies and voids.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Large-scale structure: the cosmic web**

Galaxy surveys show that matter is not distributed uniformly. It forms a cosmic web: filaments, walls, clusters, and voids. Small early density perturbations grow because overdense regions accumulate more matter. Growth is not purely spherical. Tidal structure channels infall, producing filaments and sheets.

Simulations using gravity and the observed initial fluctuation spectrum reproduce this web-like architecture. Gravity-only simulations reproduce the dark-matter web. Visible galaxy populations require baryonic physics: cooling, star formation, feedback, and gas dynamics. The pattern is therefore not an artistic metaphor. It is the characteristic outcome of gravitational growth once anchoring becomes viable.

</div>

# Galaxies: Stable Anchored Realizations

Once gravity organizes anchored space, not all regions stabilize equally. Some regions admit dense, self-reinforcing anchoring. Others do not. Galaxies are regions where anchoring succeeds at astrophysical scale.

## Galaxies Are Regimes, Not Clumps

A galaxy is not defined merely by the quantity of matter it contains. It is not defined solely by collapse, nor by cooling gas, nor by attraction as a force. Ordinarily, a galaxy is a bound system of stars, gas, dust, and dark matter. In closure terms, it is also a stable realization regime. It is a region where:

- identity anchoring becomes dense,

- curvature provides a stable scaffold,

- disturbance remains low enough for persistence to accumulate,

- and transport circulates without dissolving anchors.

Galaxies are objects in the ordinary observational sense. They are also regions where realization stabilizes.

## Anchoring Self-Reinforcement

Once anchoring density rises beyond a threshold, it reinforces itself. Persistent mass distributions deepen potential wells. Cooling and dynamical relaxation stabilize the local scaffold. A stable scaffold lowers disruption. Lower disruption permits further anchoring.

This feedback sustains structure. A galaxy is one major astrophysical regime in which this reinforcement can persist against disturbance and entropy growth.

## Transport Within Anchored Structure

Transport does not disappear inside galaxies.

Photons propagate. Energy redistributes. Gas flows. Radiation carries energy. Cosmic rays move through fields. Angular momentum is transported. Feedback reshapes the medium.

What changes is hierarchy. Transport operates within anchored structure rather than defining it. This coexistence allows galaxies to remain dynamic without losing persistence.

## Hierarchical Rotation

The same broad ingredients that stabilize rotation at galactic scale recur at smaller scales under different microphysics. Within galactic disks, molecular clouds contract under self-gravity. These clouds are never perfectly symmetric. Residual angular momentum is conserved. As contraction proceeds, material flattens into a rotating disk. Mass concentrates at the center, forming a star. Remaining material organizes into orbiting structure.

Solar systems arise from the same pattern:

- gravitational contraction,

- angular momentum conservation,

- dissipative energy loss,

- stabilization before runaway collapse.

No new principle is introduced. Hierarchy is recurrence under scale, not identity of mechanism.

## Why Galaxies Are Finite

Galaxies do not grow without bound. Beyond a certain scale, anchoring density cannot sustain reinforcement. Curvature support weakens. Angular momentum limits contraction. Virial balance stabilizes extended structure. Finite halo mass, tidal environment, mergers, feedback, gas supply, and accretion history also bound the regime.

Larger organization requires coordination between multiple anchored regions, not indefinite growth of one. Finiteness reflects stability limits, not external imposition.

## What Comes Next

Not all regions stabilize. Some never reach reinforcement. Anchors form briefly and dissolve. Transport remains dominant. These regions are voids.

Voids are not an absence of physics. They are the complementary outcome of the same constraints.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Spiral density waves: spiral arms as persistent patterns**

Spiral density wave theory provides one important concrete mechanism for how spiral arms can persist. If spiral arms were made of the same stars permanently, differential rotation would wind them up quickly. Instead, a spiral arm can be a pattern: a long-lived density enhancement with its own pattern speed. As stars and gas orbit the galactic center, they move into and out of the density wave. Gas entering the wave is compressed, which can trigger star formation. Bright young stars then light up the arms, making the pattern visible even though the material is continually changing.

Mechanically, this is persistence by throughput. The arm persists not because it is made of fixed constituents, but because the gravitational potential supports a stable wave that matter repeatedly traverses.

</div>

# Voids: Persistent Failure of Anchoring

Galaxies and voids are not opposites. They are complementary outcomes of the same structural constraints. Where anchoring reinforces itself, structure condenses. Where dense anchoring never becomes self-reinforcing, transport prevails. Voids are not empty. They are regions in which identity anchoring remains sparse at the web scale.

## Voids in the Cosmic Web

Voids arise between regions where anchoring becomes self-reinforcing: between galaxy clusters and supercluster filaments. In standard cosmology, voids are vast underdense regions, often tens of megaparsecs across, embedded within the cosmic web. They are not small gaps. They are large-scale partitions. Dense anchoring fails in these regions because density never crosses the reinforcement threshold.

Gravitational instability amplifies small differences. Regions slightly overdense grow denser. Regions slightly underdense become emptier. Voids form where web-scale reinforcement never begins.

## Differential Expansion

Expansion has different effective outcomes in dense and underdense regions. In regions where curvature wells deepen, structure stabilizes and separation grows more slowly. In regions of low density, no stabilizing basin forms. Separation grows more freely. Underdense regions show faster effective expansion and coherent outflows relative to denser structures not because space behaves differently, but because insufficient anchoring exists to resist dispersion into the background flow.

This differential growth produces the filamentary architecture of the cosmic web.

## What Defines a Void

A void is not defined by the absence of matter, but by the absence of sustained anchoring. Anchors may form transiently, but they do not reinforce one another. No persistent curvature basin develops. Dark matter remains present, yet at densities too low to sustain bound halo formation or deep potential wells capable of organizing visible structure. Global bookkeeping remains intact. Local persistence remains sparse.

## Stability of Voids

Once large-scale anchoring remains sparse, the underdensity tends to persist unless environmental dynamics alter it. Expansion further lowers density. Curvature weakens. Reinforcement remains absent. Voids persist because local conditions do not cross back into the anchoring regime.

They are stable outcomes under low anchoring density.

## Voids and Late-Time Evolution

Voids foreshadow the late universe. As anchoring thins globally, more regions resemble voids. Curvature basins weaken. Expansion dominates over reinforcement. Voids locally resemble the late-time regime assigned to dark energy: reduced spatial extendability and diminished anchoring density.

## Toward the Next Boundary

In voids, anchoring does not lock. In horizons, spatial bookkeeping itself terminates. That boundary is the horizon.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Zel’dovich approximation: anisotropic collapse and void formation**

Early density fluctuations are not perfectly spherical. Gravitational collapse proceeds anisotropically. In the Zel’dovich approximation, matter first collapses along one axis, forming sheets. Then along a second axis, forming filaments. Collapse along the third axis forms compact halos.

Large-scale regions that are slightly underdense expand along all three directions. They do not collapse as regions. Over time, these underdense regions evacuate matter toward the forming sheets and filaments. Voids therefore emerge naturally as the complement of anisotropic collapse.

Mechanically, voids are not empty gaps. They are the regions where collapse never begins at the web scale, even though smaller substructures can exist inside them.

</div>

# Horizons: Admissibility Boundaries

Black holes establish that spatial bookkeeping can terminate locally. Horizons generalize this fact. In general relativity, a horizon is a causal boundary. Here that causal boundary is read as an admissibility boundary. A horizon is an admissibility boundary: a limit beyond which spatial extension cannot be continued within one coherent exterior description.

## What a Horizon Is

A horizon marks the point at which spatial extension ceases to be jointly reconcilable. Beyond it, no single exterior ledger or static chart keeps both sides jointly recoverable. Local realization may persist. Exterior comparability does not. Crossing the boundary preserves local passage. Exterior accessibility fails because extension fails.

## Temporal and Spatial Asymmetry

Horizons reveal an asymmetry between time and space.

In time bookkeeping:

- ordering continues locally,

- irreversible bookkeeping persists,

- interior histories unfold.

In space bookkeeping:

- extension beyond the boundary fails,

- joint representation becomes impossible.

Time does not end locally at a horizon. Spatial comparability fails for the exterior ledger.

## Local Horizons

Local horizons arise when spatial bookkeeping terminates in a finite region. These are black hole horizons. They occur where compactness and causal structure produce trapped regions: paths that once connected outward can no longer return to the exterior ledger. Interior continuation remains locally admissible. What fails is shared description.

Local horizons are bounded and associated with compact mass-energy, though the horizon of a very large black hole need not have extreme local curvature.

## Global Horizons

Global horizons arise differently. They do not require local compaction. They appear when spatial extension cannot remain globally coherent under expansion and thinning. Regions beyond such a boundary are not destroyed. They are no longer jointly accessible within a shared spatial ledger.

Local realization persists. Global reach does not. A particle horizon bounds what could have influenced us so far. A cosmological event horizon, when sustained acceleration produces one, bounds what can ever reach or be reached in the future. Black hole horizons and apparent horizons mark different causal structures again.

## What Horizons Do

Horizons are not material signal blockers. They mark the loss of admissible causal paths to the exterior description. Once a region lies beyond a horizon, the continuation paths that once connected it are no longer extendable within one description. No force intervenes. No message is intercepted.

Accessibility ends because extension cannot be maintained.

## Irreversibility at the Boundary

Horizons enforce irreversibility by restricting extension. They do not create the arrow of time. They operate within it. When extension cannot be restored, irreversibility becomes unavoidable for that effective exterior description. This is not dissipation. It is reduction of admissible continuation.

## Limits and Scope

Horizons describe boundaries. They do not explain why expansion occurs or why thinning proceeds. They mark where extension ends, not what drives evolution. Some horizons arise from local termination. Others arise from global thinning.

The next chapter returns to the local extreme, where spatial bookkeeping terminates by compaction, and shows how this local boundary reshapes what remains accessible.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Particle horizon: the current limit on what can have influenced us**

The particle horizon is the concrete mechanism by which the observable universe is bounded. This is one horizon type, distinct from black hole horizons and late-time event horizons. Because light travels at a finite speed and the universe has a finite age, there is a maximum distance from which signals could have reached us by now. Regions beyond that distance are more than unseen. They have not been in causal contact with us.

This boundary is not a wall in space. It is a current limit on shared admissibility: only events within the past light cone can participate in one coherent ledger with us. Horizons are physical constraints on which histories can be mutually consistent inside the current past light cone.

</div>

# Black Holes: Termination of Spatial Bookkeeping

A horizon is the point where comparison fails: two domains can no longer be held inside one shared external ledger. Voids mark regions where anchoring persistently fails while space remains extendable. Black holes mark a different limit: the point at which spatial bookkeeping itself can no longer be extended.

## From Anchoring Failure to Spatial Failure

In voids, anchoring does not stabilize. Transport dominates, yet spatial extension remains admissible. Black holes are not the continuation of a void. They are the opposite limit: overconcentration. They appear when compaction forces a horizon, so that spatial bookkeeping cannot be continued as a single shared external ledger.

Matter need not fail.

Extendability fails for the outside description.

## What a Black Hole Is

A black hole is not an ordinary material-surface object. It is more than a concentration of matter. It is distinct from a singularity. In ideal general relativity, the event horizon defines the black-hole region. Observationally, black holes are inferred through compact mass, orbital dynamics, accretion, lensing, and horizon-scale images.

The singular limit is what classical continuation often predicts beyond it. The horizon is the boundary at which spatial bookkeeping terminates for an external description. Beyond this boundary, no unified exterior spatial ledger remains admissible for joint comparison. What ends is shared spatial comparability, not existence.

## Complementarity at the Boundary

This boundary is asymmetric.

In time bookkeeping:

- ordering continues locally,

- identities persist along their own worldlines,

- irreversible bookkeeping remains defined.

In space bookkeeping:

- compatibility channels terminate,

- extension beyond the boundary becomes inadmissible,

- interior and exterior cannot be jointly represented in the exterior ledger.

Time does not stop at a horizon. What ceases is joint spatial comparability inside one external ledger.

## Horizon and Singularity

Two limits must be distinguished. A horizon is a boundary of exterior admissibility. Interior and exterior cannot be held in one coherent external ledger. Local continuation remains intact. A singular limit, if reached, is a boundary of local admissibility.

It is where the criteria that made a local chart reusable stop closing: no neighborhood remains in which curvature, clock rates, and spatial separation can be jointly stabilized into a coherent description. Classically this appears as geodesic incompleteness, often with curvature divergence depending on the solution. Initial data cease to generate a well-posed continuation. In admissibility terms, the singularity is not treated as an ordinary place inside. It is the boundary where no continuation preserves the criteria that made description possible.

There is no stable notion of nearby, no consistent way to compare directions, and no finite refinement that restores coherence. The horizon is a projection boundary. It blocks joint exterior representation. The singularity is a realizability boundary. It blocks continuation.

A singularity should therefore not be pictured as a physical object waiting at the center. It is a failure mark in the classical continuation: the point where the description no longer supplies a reusable local neighborhood. What exists beyond that mark, if anything, belongs to whatever replaces the classical bookkeeping.

Whatever replaces the classical picture must restore a locally admissible continuation, or show that local continuation is not a demand that can be satisfied under finite description.

## Why Extension Fails

As compactness produces trapped-region structure, exterior recoverability becomes increasingly constrained. Eventually, no admissible reorganization preserves a single shared exterior ledger. Local continuation may persist. Exterior comparability does not.

## Information

Semiclassical quantum field theory predicts Hawking radiation that is nearly thermal. For known astrophysical black holes, this radiation has not been directly observed. If it were purely thermal, a pure history would become mixed. That would violate unitarity. That is the paradox.

One useful distinction follows. Information preservation is not the same as information accessibility. A horizon forbids a single exterior description from holding interior and exterior in one ledger. The correlations that preserve purity need not vanish. They can be displaced into relations that the outside chart cannot jointly recover.

The radiation can therefore look thermal while still carrying correlations. In modern island and restricted-algebra calculations, controlled models can recover a unitary Page curve. The reading here follows the same distinction: purity may be preserved even when exterior accessibility is lost.

## Toward Global Boundaries

Local spatial termination is the focus. Global boundaries arise differently: not through compaction, but through thinning of global extendability. Here termination is created by overconcentration. In the late-time reconstruction, inaccessibility emerges even without collapse, as the universe loses the ability to keep distant domains jointly admissible. Both are limits of admissibility. Only the mechanism and the scale differ.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Black hole entropy: area as boundary bookkeeping**

Black holes possess entropy proportional to the area of their horizon, not their volume. This is the Bekenstein–Hawking relation: the entropy scales with surface area. The area scaling is established. The microscopic accounting is theory-dependent. In the admissibility reading, the number of compatible exterior microconfigurations is controlled by a boundary.

The interior degrees of freedom are not independently readable. The horizon carries the externally admissible bookkeeping.

Black hole entropy is a concrete example of bookkeeping constrained by boundary extension, not by interior volume.

</div>

# Dark Energy: Global Capacity Exhaustion

Inflation was the first historical response to excessive realization density. Dark energy is the late-time counterpart in this arc. They express the same constraint encountered at opposite limits. The same structural interpretation introduced earlier now returns as a late-time historical regime.

## Symmetry with Inflation

At the onset of coordinated realization, density was too high. Spatial extension could not remain coherent without rapid dilution. Inflation is the historical regime in which global extension increases rapidly enough to restore admissibility under overload. At late times, the inverse condition emerges. The cosmic mean density thins. Persistent identity remains locally bound, but becomes sparse at the largest scale.

The consequence is loss of global comparability. Continuation thins beyond what a shared ledger can hold. In capacity language, dark energy names the regime in which this thinning crosses a stability threshold under global capacity limits.

## After Matter Domination

During matter domination, anchoring density was high. Gravity organized persistent identity effectively. Extension and ordering remained stable. As expansion proceeds, the mean cosmic matter density decreases. Bound systems can persist locally, but matter becomes sparse across the large-scale expansion. Large-scale coherence weakens.

Eventually, the limiting factor is no longer local anchoring stability, but global extendability itself. In the Friedmann description, this is when the effective dark-energy component becomes dynamically dominant.

## A Constraint, Not a Substance

No new local field or material substance is introduced here. In standard cosmology, late-time acceleration is described by a cosmological constant or vacuum energy term. Current surveys keep other descriptions active: evolving dark energy, scalar-field models, modified gravity, and effective equations of state. The observational description remains. The interpretation changes.

Dark energy names the structural limit at which global spatial extension can no longer be maintained without increasing separation. No local ingredient is inserted. A boundary condition is reached.

## Why Acceleration Occurs

The reconstruction reads acceleration this way: when global extension can no longer remain coherent under thinning anchoring, two broad resolutions remain: loss of anchored structure, increase of separation between anchored regions. The first corresponds to loss of persistence. The second preserves persistence by relaxing global comparability. Acceleration is the large-scale response when global extension would otherwise lose admissibility. Separation grows because coherence cannot be maintained otherwise.

## Dark Energy and Horizons

Under sustained Lambda-CDM-like acceleration, a cosmological event horizon becomes part of the future structure. In the capacity reading, dark energy is the regime in which approach to that boundary becomes unavoidable. As expansion accelerates, the set of jointly extendable continuations shrinks. Distant regions remain locally real, but cease to be recoverable within one external ledger. The horizon does not cause the regime. It is what the regime makes unavoidable.

## The Fine-Tuning Question

Standard equations allow a cosmological constant. Observations say it is small. That smallness is the puzzle. Naive accounting of zero-point contributions suggests an enormous scale. The observed scale is not.

The number and timing remain empirical inputs. What changes is how they are read. Instead of treating the cosmological constant as a sum that failed to cancel, treat it as a boundary whose effect was hidden while anchoring dominated. While anchoring dominates, the boundary is inert. When the universe becomes dilute, it becomes active.

The number remains. The interpretation shifts: not as miraculous cancellation, but as the scale at which global compatibility begins to limit extension. The puzzle changes shape.

## The Mirror of the Beginning

Inflation and dark energy frame the cosmic arc. Inflation resolves excessive density through rapid expansion. Dark energy resolves thinning anchoring through accelerated separation.

One dilutes overload.

The other dilutes accessibility. They form a structural rhyme, not an identity of mechanism. Follow that constraint forward. Transport begins to dominate again.

<div class="framed">

**Mechanism: Type Ia supernovae: the observation that forced accelerated expansion**

Type Ia supernovae provide the concrete observational evidence behind cosmic acceleration. These explosions have a characteristic light curve shape that allows them to be used as standardizable candles. Their intrinsic brightness can be inferred from how the light curve evolves. By comparing inferred brightness to observed brightness, one obtains distances. Redshift provides the expansion factor.

The measured distance–redshift relation does not match a universe that is only decelerating under ordinary matter and radiation. Distant supernovae appear dimmer than expected in a purely decelerating model, indicating that expansion has accelerated over cosmic history.
